Actual model-written shot

Overview: Hyperrealistic live-action creature cinematography, natural materials, luminous elemental effects and grounded weight. The red trainer sends out Charizard in one continuous static shot.

Near team: LOWER-LEFT quadrant, centered x25% y70%, rear three-quarter facing upper-right: empty Pokémon space at the opening. The red-jacket trainer stands directly behind it holding one closed red-and-white Poké Ball containing Charizard. Recognizable orange scaled dragon, cream belly, two horns, two arms, powerful hind legs, teal leathery wings and flame-tipped tail. Photographic reptile skin and translucent wing veins. The creature remains inside the closed ball at the opening.

Far team: UPPER-RIGHT quadrant, centered x76% y32%, front three-quarter facing lower-left: empty Pokémon space. The blue-jacket trainer waits behind it holding a closed Poké Ball. This battle position remains empty throughout the shot.

Camera and environment: Fixed 35mm camera pitched downward 65 degrees. Ground fills the frame; horizon and sky stay above the crop. Red is near-left foreground; blue is far-right background, several body lengths farther from the lens. Keep open diagonal ground between them. Rugged woodland floor, moss, sunlit mist and a clear diagonal attack lane.

Action: Within the first second, the red-jacket trainer on the LEFT (S1), with a youthful bright mid-pitched voice, calls out exactly once, mouth open in sync: <d>[English] Charizard, I choose you!</d> The blue-jacket trainer listens silently with mouth closed. As the line finishes, S1's mouth closes and the trainer throws one red-and-white Poké Ball toward their own empty lower-left battle position. One ball throw swish, then an opening click and a brief white flash. Exactly one Charizard emerges from the shimmering light, wings spreading, and lands by approximately three seconds with a grounded thump of feet on mossy earth. It plants its hind legs, settles into a confident ready stance, tail flame flickering, and holds through the end. The opposite team stays unchanged. No attack, damage or evolution. One continuous static shot at natural speed. At most two Pokémon, one per occupied side. No duplicate bodies.

The shot ends with the same locked composition, both Pokémon alert and still except for breathing and the small tail flame.

The Gyarados dissolves gradually into flowing crimson light that streams smoothly back across the short distance, shrinking the great coils, fins and whiskers until the last glimmer pours into the ball around the four-second mark. In the final second the ball's halves close with a soft click and the trainer holds it still at chest height, remaining kneeling on the red side. Throughout, Pikachu waits alert on its own side with feet planted, ears giving one small attentive twitch, and the blue-jacket trainer stands quietly behind it. No words are spoken; every mouth stays closed.

For example: Finn (S1), with a youthful, clear, energetic voice, says: <d>[English] spoken words</d>. Describe another character's action in a separate sentence so the listener cannot be mistaken for the speaker.

Silent creatures may open their mouths when the accepted move requires it, such as breathing fire. Vocal silence does not require a creature’s mouth to stay closed. The supplied line is the only speech in the shot; do not add ad-libbed words, background conversation, or narration.
